Let's design the greedy construction robustly:
```
def greedy_build(n, M, a0):
    a = np.zeros(n)
    if a0 <0 or a0*a0 > M+eps: return None
    a[0]=a0
    b = np.zeros(2*n-1)
    b[0]=a0*a0
    for i in range(1,n):
        # current b values affected by a_i: b[i:i+i] (pairs with r=0..i-1), plus square b[2i]
        lim = M
        if i>0:
            bseg = b[i:i+i]
            r = 2*a[:i]
            # for each r, require 2 x a_r <= M - bseg -> x <= (M-bseg)/r
            # If any M-bseg <=0 -> infeasible unless a_r=0 (but r>0)
            rem = M - bseg
            # x0 = min(rem / r)
            # Use np.minimum.reduce? r length i. rem maybe negatives.
            # Avoid divide by zero; r positive.
            # To handle negative rem quickly:
            max_x = M # initial by square? 
            # linear:
            ratios = rem / r
            x = np.min(ratios)
            # square:
            if 2*i < len(b):
                rem_sq = M - b[2*i]
                if rem_sq < 0: infeasible
                x = min(x, sqrt(rem_sq))
            if x < 0: infeasible
            a[i]=x
            if x>0:
                b[i:i+i] += 2*x*a[:i]
                if 2*i < len(b): b[2*i] += x*x
                # due floats, clip b? maybe small exced; if b > M + tol break
        # stop if sum? No, need fill all? If remaining a_i=0 maybe break; sum won't change.
        # But we need length n for score? We can fill zeros.
    return a
```
If sum_a<1, we can scale? Scaling up increases b by factor; if scale factor s, max b becomes s^2 M; to score with sum1, if original sum S<1, normalized max = M/S^2 >M, score >2nM/ S^2. We need S≥1 for guarantee. If S close, we can try increase M or adjust a0.

We can also after greedy, normalize and evaluate actual score. The actual max may be ≤M by construction; if sum<1, normalized score =2n*(M/S^2) maybe. We can track. Efficient pair_score(δ):
- Unaffected max: We can precompute `c` and `cmax`. For δ=0, unaffected max = cmax if cmax not in affected indices; else second max. Affected coefficient k if term with i or j:
- k = i + r for r=0..n-1 (r valid): coefficient c_{i+r} has term a_i a_r and a_r a_i? Actually both orientations if r≠i. Changing a_i affects c_{i+r} (2δ a_r + δ² if i=r). This set is i..i+n-1.
- Changing a_j affects c_{j+r}, r=0..n-1: j..j+n-1.
Union length up to 2n. But some overlap. So affected O(2n), almost full. Recopy full c for each δ may be similar to recompute? But we can just copy c and update two slices O(n) and max.     # Check descent for proximal: phi(x) <= phi(y) + dot(g,x-y) + 0.5/step*||x-y||^2
    if condition or iters<...: break
    step*=0.5
```

FISTA momentum:
```
t=1
while:
  t_new=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4t^2)); mom=(t_new-1)/t; y=proj(a + mom*(a-prev));
  c_y=conv_fft(y); phi_y?; g=grad_weighted(y,p_y)
  backtracking line search using phi_y and y; a_new=...
  if score improved update best.
  prev=a; a=a_new; t=t_new
```

Let's design pair coordinate search in detail and maybe use it as primary after a good start. It can directly minimize max coefficient, nonconvex but local.

Pair selection and update algorithm:
- Maintain a (sum1), c, cmax.
- Use a priority of candidate pairs generated from top coefficients.
- For each candidate pair (i,j), perform line search to find best δ in interval that minimizes max. Accept if score improves by >tol; update a,c (incremental).
- Repeat.

How to line search robustly/fast:
Function `pair_eval(a,c,i,j,delta)` returns new c max? We can compute delta_array and max.
We can use golden-section over δ interval [-a_j, a_i]. But if function nonunimodal, golden may fail. We can combine with coarse sampling: evaluate at 0, ±r for r in [0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75] times min(a_i,a_j)? Also direction to reduce products: If k=i+j high and a_i>a_j, candidate δ = (a_j-a_i)*frac (negative). Use these points and choose best; then refine around best with ternary if needed.
To save time, for each pair do 7-10 evaluations, no full refinements.

But choosing pairs: If we random pair, many not affect top coefficients. We can select from high c. For top K coefficients (e.g., K=100), for each k, choose a few pairs. The pair (i,k-i). ```
def pair_polish(a, budget):
   a=normalize(a.copy()); c=conv(a); best=...
   # Precompute top coefficients updates after each acceptance.
   for it in range until deadline:
      topK = 20
      top_idx = argpartition(c,-topK)
      candidates=[]
      for k in top_idx:
         lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
         # choose a few i with high product and imbalance
         # To vectorize, rng? We can sample random 10 indices in [lo,hi)
         for sample:
            i=sample; j=k-i; if i!=j:
                # try base directions
                base=(a[i]-a[j])/2.0
                for frac in [0.5,1.0, -0.2?]: # base sign direction to make more unequal
                   cand=trial(a,c,i,j,base*frac)
                   if cand<best: update
      # Also random pair global? 
      if no improvement many, change topK or random pairs.
```
Trial function:
```
oldi,oldj = a[i],a[j]
# Ensure delta within [-oldj, oldi]
if delta<=0: delta=max(delta,-oldj*0.999) else delta=min(delta,oldi*0.999)
# delta vector:
newc = c.copy()
newc[i:i+n] += 2*delta*a[:n]  # but using old a; if j in range, will include? formula uses old a, okay
newc[j:j+n] -= 2*delta*a[:n]
newc[i+i] += delta*delta
newc[j+j] += delta*delta
newc[i+j] -= 2*delta*delta
# Need clips for boundaries: slices i:i+n may exceed len? len_c=2n-1, i+n-1 max for i=n-1 =>2n-2 good. start i >=0.
# But formula 2δ a_{k-i} for k in [i,i+n-1] includes a_{k-i}. For newc[i:i+n] yes a[0:n]. For k=j? subtract.
# However if i+j index receives both linear and quadratic handled by subtract 2δ².
score = 2*n*newc.max() (sum unchanged 1)
if score<best: accept; update a[i],a[j], c=newc (copied)
```
This copies c for each trial; 9611 floats. To speed, we can preallocate `newc` and copy. Use `c.copy()`.

    delta = frac * (larger-smaller? direction?)
```

- `convolve_fft(a)`:
```
n=len(a); size=1; while size < 2*n-1: size*=2
A=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,size)[:2*n-1]
c=np.maximum(c,0) # But negative tiny; also due FFT error maybe max small negative? max okay
c[-1]=? # no issue
return c
```
For gradient convolution a*w_rev length up to 3n-3, size needs >=3n-3. We can use `Ngrad=1; while Ngrad<3*n-3: Ngrad*=2`. For n=4806, 16384. For conv c, 16384 too. Use same N if Ngrad enough. Precompute size in object? Top-level no closures; can pass.
- `active_gradient(a, idx)`:
```
n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
for k in idx:
    k=int(k); lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
    if lo<hi:
       r=np.arange(lo,hi)
       g[lo:hi] += 2*a[k-r]
g /= len(idx)
return g
```
Vectorization inside loop with arange each time; active maybe 100-500; allocations heavy. We can precompute ranges? For n fixed, for each k, lo/hi and indices. But k changes. Could make active gradient with FFT for large top_k to avoid Python loops.
FFT gradient:
```
def fft_gradient(a,w,N): # w length 2n-1 dense
   wr = w[::-1]
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); W=np.fft.rfft(wr,N)
   corr=np.fft.irfft(A*W,N)
   s=corr[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]
   return 2*s
```
If top_k small (<128), active gradient; else FFT. For FFT, need w dense. Build w zeros and set `w[idx]=1/top_k`. This allocation 9611 each iter okay. If active count large, FFT 2 rfft+irfft. For top_k=512, active loop 512 slices maybe ~2.5M vector ops; could be comparable; FFT likely faster. Use threshold 256.
- `softmax_weights(c, temp)`:
```
m=c.max(); z=np.exp((c-m)/temp); s=z.sum(); return z/s, m + temp*np.log(s)
```
If temp very small, z all zero except max (underflow) but sum okay. If many max equal? okay.

Active gradient function with selected `idx` and uniform weights. For softmax weights, use FFT.

`refine_active(a0, duration, rng, init_step=1.0, init_top=32, use_soft=True)`:
Let's detail a robust loop:
```
def refine_active(a0, duration, rng, top_start=32, top_max=1024, temp_m=0.01):
    n=len(a); a=normalize(a0); prev=a.copy(); t=1.0
    best=best_array(a); best_score=score; score_best=...
    c_best=conv...
    step=1.0; top=top_start; temp_m_here=temp_m
    deadline=... ; it=0; stall=0; no_improve=0
    momentum=0.9
    while time<deadline:
        # every 500 iters maybe adjust
        t_new=...
        mom=(t_new -1)/t
        y = a + mom*(a-prev)
        np.maximum(y,0,out=y)
        sy=y.sum()
        if sy<1e-15: y=a.copy()
        else: y=project_simplex(y) # ensure sum1
        c=convolve_fft(y); smax=c.max(); score_y=2*n*smax
        # choose weights
        if use_rotating: maybe top average
        # To avoid symmetry, always include top_k and maybe random k? If top set symmetric and sequence near symmetric, add small random coefficients? But top apply. Could use softmax with temp to include noncentral weights, providing nonconstant gradient even for symmetric? For uniform, softmax over triangular includes tail lower values; weighted gradient may be nonconstant due boundaries? Let's see uniform c triangular. Weights proportional to c (temp large). Gradient =2∑w_k a_{k-i}. Since a constant, g_i=2 const * number of valid k with weight? The number of valid k for each i near boundaries differs, so gradient nonconstant! Good. Softmax with broad weights can move uniform. Top-center only constant. Use softmax with moderate temp for exploration.
        temp = max(1e-9, temp_m * smax)
        if temp_m > 1e-5:
           p, phi = softmax_weights(c,temp)
           g=fft_gradient(y,p,N)
        else:
           top_idx=...
           g=active_gradient(y, top_idx)
        # Backtracking line search for exact score (not smooth condition)
        cand=None; cand_score=None
        for _ in range(6):
           cand = project_simplex(y - step*g)
           c_new=convolve_fft(cand); sc=2*n*c_new.max()
           if sc < score_y:
               cand_score=sc; break
           step *= 0.5
        if cand is None or cand_score is None:
           # revert, reduce step, reset momentum
           step = max(0.1, step*0.5)
           prev = a.copy(); t=1
           # maybe add small noise if stuck
           if no_improve>1000:
               y2 = best + rng.normal(0,0.001/sqrt(n),n); y2=normalize; a=y2?;
           continue
        a_new=cand
        if cand_score < best_score:
           best_score=cand_score; best_array=a_new.copy(); no_improve=0; step=min(3,step*1.1)
        else:
           no_improve+=1
           step=max(0.05, step*0.8)
        a=a_new; t=t_new; prev=prev? (prev was old a)

Let's write `project_simplex` robust:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n=v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.arange(1,n+1)
    cond = u*rho > (cssv - 1)
    theta = (cssv[np.where(cond)[0].max()] -1)/ (max_rho?)...
```
Implement standard:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n=len(v)
    if n==0: return v.copy()
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1)
    cond = u*idx > cssv - 1
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    k = np.where(cond)[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[k] - 1.0)/(k+1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
This returns sum≈1.

`normalize(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float).copy(); a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s<=0: return uniform; return a/s
```

Now, active_gradient with idx array. To improve speed, if idx length > 200 use FFT. But active_gradient only called in gradient_refine when we choose top. We can have `compute_gradient(a, c, top_k)` returns g:
```
def compute_gradient(a, c, top_k):
   n=len(a)
   if top_k >= c.size: idx=np.arange(c.size)
   else:
      idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
   if top_k <= 200:
      return active_gradient(a, idx), idx
   else:
      w=np.zeros(c.size); w[idx]=1.0/top_k
      N=next_power2(3*n-4)
      return fft_gradient(a,w,N), idx
```

```
def pair_polish(a0, duration, rng):
    a=normalize(a0); c=conv(a); best_score=...; best_a=a.copy()
    deadline=...; iter=0; no=0; accepted_count=0
    while time<deadline:
       # choose top 16 coeff
       topK=16
       top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -topK)[-topK:]
       # Generate candidates dictionary
       tried=set(); cand_videos=[]
       for k in top_idx:
          lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
          if hi-lo <2: continue
          idx_local=np.arange(lo,hi)
          prod=a[idx_local]*a[k-idx_local]
          # top 4 product
          m=min(4, len(prod))
          if m>0:
             loc=np.argpartition(prod,-m)[-m:]
             samples=idx_local[loc]
          else: samples=[]
          # add 2 random
          if len(idx_local)>0:
             samples=np.concatenate([samples, idx_local[rng.integers(0,len(idx_local),2)]]) maybe duplicate
          for i in samples:
             i=int(i); j=k-i
             if j<0 or j>=n or i==j: continue
             key=(min(i,j),max(i,j))
             if key in tried: continue
             tried.add(key); cand_videos.append((i,j))
          if iter%? break cap 20
       # Also if no candidates, random pairs
       if len(cand_videos)<5:
          for _ in range(20): i,j = rng.choice? sample distinct
          cand_videos.append((i,j))
       improved=False
       for i,j in cand_videos[:30]:
          # choose delta candidates
          x,yv = a[i],a[j]; diff=x-yv
          max_amt=min(x,yv)
          # If diff zero, use random +/- max_amt*0.3
          fracs=(0.3,0.7,1.0)
          best_trial=None
          for frac in fracs:
             for sgn in (1,-1):
                if abs(diff)>1e-15:
                   delta = sgn*diff*frac
                else:
                   delta = sgn*max_amt*frac
                # clamp
                if delta > x: delta=x*0.99
                if delta < -yv: delta=-yv*0.99
                if abs(delta) < 1e-13: continue
                trial...
                if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                   if best_trial is None or sc<best_trial[0]: best_trial=(sc, delta, c_trial)
          if best_trial:
             sc,delta,ctr=best_trial
             a[i]+=delta; a[j]-=delta
             c=ctr.copy(); current? We are best-accepting; update best.
             # If a sum drif? no.
             improved=True
             # After accept, recompute top; break out to regenerate candidates
             if (accepted_count%50==0): c=conv(a)
             accepted_count+=1
             break
       if not improved:
          no+=1
          if no>300: # restart from best perturbed
             a=best_a + rng.normal(0, 0.005/np.sqrt(n), n); a=normalize(a); c=conv(a); no=0
             if accepted_count%? 
       iter+=1
       if iter%100==0: print
    return best_a,best_score
```

Let's draft code in mind:
```
import numpy as np, time, math

def next_power2(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p

def normalize(a): ...

def project_simplex(v): ...

def convolve_fft(a):
    n=len(a)
    need=2*n-1
    N=next_power2(need)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)
    return np.maximum(c[:need],0.0)
```
Calling `next_power2` each conv might be overhead; but okay. Could cache by n in global dict? Top-level no closure? We can use global variable `_N_CACHE={}`. But in code contest, okay. Simpler compute with bit_length:
`N = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()`.
Use that.

`score_array`:
```
def score_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size==0: return inf, None
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: return inf, None
    a /= s
    c=convolve_fft(a)
    return float(2*a.size*c.max()), c
```

`fft_gradient(a,w,N)`:
```
def fft_gradient(a,w,N):
    n=len(a)
    ar=np.zeros(w.size); ar[::-1]? Actually w_rev = w[::-1]
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); W=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(A*W,N)
    s=corr[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]
    g=2.0*s
    g[np.abs(g)<1e-15]=0.0
    return g
```
Need if N maybe less than corr indices? N from 3n-3, okay.

`active_gradient(a, idx)`:
```
def active_gradient(a, idx):
    n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k); lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
       if lo<hi:
          r=np.arange(lo,hi,dtype=np.int64)
          g[lo:hi] += 2.0*a[k-r]
    return g / float(max(1,len(idx)))
```
If idx length 0 return zeros.

`compute_gradient(a,c,top_k,N)`:
```
def compute_gradient(a,c,top_k,N):
   L=c.size
   if top_k>=L: idx=np.arange(L)
   else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
   if top_k <= 80:
       return active_gradient(a, idx)
   w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0/top_k
   return fft_gradient(a,w,N)
```
Need if `np.argpartition` with top_k=0? no.

`gradient_refine(a0, deadline, rng, top_start=16, top_max=4096):`
Implement as discussed.

Let's write detailed code:
```
def gradient_refine(a0, deadline, rng, top_start=16, top_max=4096):
    n=len(a0)
    a=normalize(a0)
    c=convolve_fft(a); cur_score=float(2*n*c.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    prev=a.copy(); beta=0.0; accepts_in_row=0
    top_k=top_start; scale=0.01; no_best=0
    N=1 << (3*n - 3).bit_length()
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Build y
        y=a
        if beta > 0.0:
            y = a + beta*(a-prev)
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)): y=a
            else:
                y = project_simplex(y)
                # if y too far? evaluate score_y? not now
        c_y=convolve_fft(y)
        score_y=float(2*n*c_y.max())
        # If y score worse than current by >1e-6, revert y=a and c_y=cur_c maybe to save
        if score_y > cur_score + 1e-8:
            y=a.copy()
            c_y=cur_c.copy()
            score_y=cur_score
        gmax = 0.0
        # Need gradient; if c_y max=0? no.
        g=compute_gradient(y, c_y, top_k, N)
        gmax=float(g.max()) if g.size else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax < 1e-15:
            # reset
            beta=0.0; no_best=min(no_best+1,10**9); continue
        step = scale/(gmax+1e-12)
        accepted=False
        for _ in range(5):
            cand = project_simplex(y - step*g)
            # Ensure nonneg (project does)
            c_cand = convolve_fft(cand)
            sc = float(2*n*c_cand.max())
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                # accept
                prev = a.copy()
                a = cand
                cur_c = c_cand
                cur_score = sc
                accepted=True
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score=sc; best=a.copy(); no_best=0
                else:
                    no_best+=1
                break
            step *= 0.5
        if accepted:
            accepts_in_row +=1
            beta = min(0.5, beta + 0.02 if accepts_in_row>=3 else 0.0)
            scale = min(0.2, scale*1.05)
        else:
            accepts_in_row=0
            beta = max(0.0, beta*0.5)
            scale = max(1e-5, scale*0.6)
            # no change; if long no accept, perturb
            no_best += 1
        it +=1
        if it % 500 ==0:
            if no_best > 100:
                top_k=min(top_max, top_k*2)
                scale=max(scale,0.02)
                beta=0.0
                prev=a.copy()
                if no_best > 2000:
                    noise=rng.normal(0,0.005/np.sqrt(n),n)
                    a=normalize(best + noise)
                    cur_c=convolve_fft(a); cur_score=float(2*n*cur_c.max())
                    prev=a.copy(); top_k=max(top_start, top_k//2)
                    no_best=500
            if it % 2000 ==0:
                print(f"  grad it={it} cur={cur_score:.8f} best={best_score:.8f} top={top_k}")
    # final if a better
    if cur_score < best_score:
       best=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    return best, float(best_score)
```
Note: When no_best reset noise, best unchanged; current may be worse. Acceptance requires improve current, not best, so it might converge to a score worse than best and stop at deadline; best remains. Good.
Potential issue: If current_score is much worse (after noise) and no accept improves to below current for long, no_best grows again; it may repeatedly noise. okay.

Maybe accepting only if `< cur_score` can be too strict due floating: use `-1e-13`. If objective plateaus, no accept, stuck. We can accept equal within tol if it changes position? Not needed.

Pair_polish code:
```
def pair_polish(a0, deadline, rng, topK=12, pairs_per=20, deltas=...):
   a=normalize(a0); c=conv(a); best_score=...; best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy()
   no=0; accepts=0; it=0
   deadline2=deadline
   while time<deadline:
      top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -topK)[-topK:]
      tried=set(); pairs=[]
      for k in top_idx:
        ... samples
      if len(pairs)<pairs_per: add random
      improved=False
      for i,j in pairs[:pairs_per]:
         x=a[i]; y=a[j]; diff=x-y; maxamt=min(x,y)
         fracs = (0.15,0.35,0.6,0.9,1.0)
         # directions: sign = 1 if diff>0 else -1 for reducing k; and opposite
         base = abs(diff) if abs(diff)>1e-14 else maxamt
         signs = (1,-1)
         best_delta=None; best_sc=None; best_cand=None
         for frac in fracs:
            for sgn in signs:
               delta = sgn*base*frac
               if diff<0? Actually base abs and sgn gives + and -: covers. If diff==0 also.
               clamp.
               # quick bound: If x+delta negative? clamp.
               newc = c.copy()
               seg = a  # old
               newc[i:i+n] += 2*delta*seg
               newc[j:j+n] -= 2*delta*seg
               newc[2*i] += delta*delta
               if 2*j < len(newc): newc[2*j] += delta*delta
               newc[i+j] -= 2*delta*delta
               sc=2*n*newc.max()
               if sc < best_score - 1e-12 and (best_sc is None or sc < best_sc):
                  best_sc=sc; best_delta=delta; best_cand=newc
         if best_delta is not None:
             a[i]+=best_delta; a[j]-=best_delta
             # recompute
             c_re=convolve_fft(a); sc_re=2*n*c_re.max()
             if sc_re < best_score - 1e-12:
                 best_score=float(sc_re); best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c_re; best_sc?; c=best_c.copy(); accepts+=1; no=0; improved=True
             else:
                 # revert a to best_a? since current a equal best_a plus delta? If best_score unchanged, we were at best? Actually a might have been best_a or perturbed. We should keep a as best_a if invalid.
                 a=best_a.copy(); c=best_c.copy()

      if not improved:
          no+=1
          if no > 300:
             # perturb best
             a=normalize(best_a + rng.normal(0,0.004/np.sqrt(n), n))
             c=convolve_fft(a); no=0
      it+=1
      if it%200==0: print
   return best_a, best_score
```

def softmax_weights(c, temp):
    m = c.max()

    s = z.sum()

def next_pow2(x):

project_simplex:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n = v.size
    if n == 0: return v.copy()
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u*idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, 1.0/n, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(np.where(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[k] - 1.0)/(k+1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
 Good.

normalize:
```
def normalize_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0: return np.ones(1)/1? But ensure length?
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    np.clip(a,0.0,None,out=a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.ones_like(a)/max(1,a.size)
    return a/s
```
If a scalar? size1 okay.

```

convolve_fft:
```
def convolve_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    need=2*n-1
    if need <=0: return np.zeros(0)
    N=1 << (need-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)[:need]
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    return c
```
For n=1, need=1, N=1, works.

fft_gradient:
```
def fft_gradient(a,w,N):
    n=a.size
    wr = w[::-1]
    need = n + w.size -1
    if N < need:
        N=1 << (need-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    W=np.fft.rfft(wr,N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(A*W,N)
    s = corr[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]
    g = 2.0*s
    g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
    return g
```
If n=1: 2n-2=0, stop n-2=-1, slice 0:-1:-1? In Python, `corr[0:-1:-1]` returns empty? For n=1, s should length1 at index0. Slice `corr[0 : -1 : -1]` starts 0, stops -1 exclusive going backwards, no elements. Need handle n=1 separately. Our n=4806, no issue. But for robustness add `if n==1: return 2*np.array([a[0]*w[0]])`? Actually w length1, g=2a0w0. Add:
```
if n == 1:
   return np.array([2.0*a[0]*w[0]])
```
Good.

active_gradient:
```
def active_gradient(a, idx):
    n=a.size; g=np.zeros(n)
    if idx.size==0: return g
    for kk in idx:
       k=int(kk)
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
       if lo<hi:
          r=np.arange(lo,hi,dtype=np.int64)
          g[lo:hi] += 2.0*a[k-r]
    return g/float(idx.size)
```
If idx is list, use np.asarray. In compute, idx np array.

compute_gradient:
```
def compute_gradient_top(a,c,top_k,N):
    L=c.size
    top_k=int(min(max(1,top_k), L))
    if top_k >= L:
       idx=np.arange(L)
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
    if top_k <= 80:
       return active_gradient(a, idx)
    w=np.zeros(L)
    w[idx]=1.0/top_k
    return fft_gradient(a,w,N)
```
For n=1, N computed 3? okay.

softmax_weights:
```
def softmax_weights(c,temp):
    m=float(c.max())
    z=np.exp((c-m)/temp)
    s=z.sum()
    if s <=0:
       w=np.zeros_like(c)
       w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
       return w
    return z/s
```

```
def gradient_refine(a0, deadline, rng, top_start=16, top_max=4096, soft_fraction=0.35, soft_start=0.04, soft_min=0.001):
    a=normalize_array(a0)
    n=a.size
    c=convolve_fft(a); cur_score=float(2.0*n*c.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    prev=a.copy(); beta=0.0; accepts=0
    top_k=top_start; scale=0.015; no_best=0
    N=1 << (3*n - 3).bit_length()
    start=time.monotonic(); soft_dead=start + (deadline-start)*soft_fraction
    soft_temp=soft_start
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if beta>0:
          y = a + beta*(a-prev)
          y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
          y = project_simplex(y)
          c_y=convolve_fft(y)
          score_y=float(2.0*n*c_y.max())
          if score_y > cur_score + 1e-8:
             y=a; c_y=c; score_y=cur_score
       else:
          y=a; c_y=c
       # choose gradient
       if time.monotonic() < soft_dead:
          temp = max(soft_min, soft_temp * max(1e-15, float(c_y.max())))
          w=softmax_weights(c_y, temp)
          g=fft_gradient(y,w,N)
       else:
          g=compute_gradient_top(y,c_y,top_k,N)
       gmax=float(np.max(g)) if g.size else 0.0
       if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax < 1e-15:
          beta=0.0; no_best+=1
          if no_best>1000: a=normalize_array(best + rng.normal(0,0.005/np.sqrt(n),n)); c=convolve_fft(a); cur_score=... no_best=0
          continue
       step=scale/(gmax+1e-12)
       accepted=False
       for _ in range(4):
          cand=project_simplex(y - step*g)
          c_cand=convolve_fft(cand)
          sc=float(2.0*n*c_cand.max())
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
             prev=a.copy(); a=cand; c=c_cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True
             if sc < best_score:
                best=a.copy(); best_score=sc; no_best=0
             else:
                no_best +=1
             break
          step *=0.5
       if accepted:
          accepts +=1
          if accepts >=3: beta=min(0.5, beta+0.02)
          scale=min(0.25, scale*1.05)
       else:
          accepts=0
          beta=max(0.0, beta*0.5)
          scale=max(1e-5, scale*0.6)
          no_best +=1
       it+=1
       if time.monotonic() < soft_dead:
          if it % 500 ==0: soft_temp=max(soft_min, soft_temp*0.7)
       if it % 200 ==0:
          if no_best > 100:
             top_k=min(top_max, top_k*2)
             scale=max(scale,0.02)
             beta=0.0
             prev=a.copy()
             if it % 500 !=0:
                pass
          if no_best > 2000:
             a=normalize_array(best + rng.normal(0,0.006/np.sqrt(n),n))
             c=convolve_fft(a); cur_score=float(2.0*n*c.max())
             prev=a.copy(); beta=0.0; top_k=max(top_start, min(top_max, top_k))
             no_best=500
          if it % 2000 ==0:
             print(f"  grad it={it} cur={cur_score:.8f} best={best_score:.8f} top={top_k} scale={scale:.5f}")
    if cur_score < best_score:
       best=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    return best, float(best_score)
```

Pair_polish code implement with robust top sampling. Use `rng.choice`? Avoid value_error if length. Use indices.
```
def pair_polish(a0, deadline, rng, topK=12, max_pairs=20):
    a=normalize_array(a0); n=a.size
    c=convolve_fft(a); best_score=2*n*c.max(); best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy()
    no=0; it=0; accepts=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if c.size ==0: break
       top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -topK)[-topK:]
       tried=[]; tried_set=set(); pairs=[]
       for kk in top_idx:
          k=int(kk)
          lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
          if hi-lo <2: continue
          ii=np.arange(lo,hi,dtype=np.int64)
          jj=k-ii
          valid=(ii>=0)&(ii<n)&(jj>=0)&(jj<n)&(ii!=jj)
          if not valid.any(): continue
          ii=ii[valid]; jj=jj[valid]
          prod=a[ii]*a[jj]
          m=min(4, ii.size)
          if m>0:
             loc=np.argpartition(prod, -m)[-m:]
             samples=ii[loc]
          else: samples=np.array([], dtype=int)
          ridx=rng.integers(0, ii.size, size=2)
          samples=np.concatenate([samples, ii[ridx]])
          for val in samples:
             i=int(val); j=int(k-i)
             if i==j or j<0 or j>=n: continue
             key=(min(i,j),max(i,j))
             if key in tried_set: continue
             tried_set.add(key); pairs.append((i,j))
          if len(pairs)>=max_pairs: break
       if len(pairs) < max_pairs:
          for _ in range(max_pairs - len(pairs)):
             i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
             if i==j: continue
             key=...; if not in tried_set: add
       if not pairs:
          no +=1
          if no>100: reset perturb
          it+=1; continue
       improved=False
       for i,j in pairs[:max_pairs]:
          x=float(a[i]); y=float(a[j])
          if x <=0 and y <=0: continue
          diff=x-y; maxamt=min(x,y)
          base=abs(diff)
          if base < 1e-12: base=maxamt*0.5
          if base <=0: continue
          fracs=(0.15,0.35,0.6,0.9,1.0)
          best_delta=None; best_sc=None; best_c=None
          for frac in fracs:
             for sgn in (1.0,-1.0):
                delta=sgn*base*frac
                if delta > x: delta=0.999*x
                if delta < -y: delta=-0.999*y
                if abs(delta) < 1e-14: continue
                newc=c.copy()
                newc[i:i+n] += 2.0*delta*a
                newc[j:j+n] -= 2.0*delta*a
                newc[2*i] += delta*delta
                if 2*j < newc.size: newc[2*j] += delta*delta
                newc[i+j] -= 2.0*delta*delta
                # due to numerical, max might be negative? no
                sc=float(2.0*n*newc.max())
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                    if best_sc is None or sc < best_sc:
                       best_sc=sc; best_delta=delta; best_c=newc
          if best_delta is not None:
             a[i] += best_delta; a[j] -= best_delta
             np.maximum(a,0.0,out=a)
             # recompute full c and true score
             c_re=convolve_fft(a); sc_re=float(2.0*n*c_re.max())
             if np.isfinite(sc_re) and sc_re < best_score - 1e-13:
                 best_score=sc_re; best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c_re.copy(); c=best_c
                 accepts +=1; no=0; improved=True
             else:
                 # revert to best to maintain a=best? If a was perturbation and trial invalid, leave a? To search around perturbed, but best-accepting we can set a to best after invalid? Setting to best loses perturbation. Maybe keep a as perturbed? But a changed by delta; if not best, we can revert delta.
                 a[i] -= best_delta; a[j] += best_delta
             break
       if not improved:
          no +=1
          if no > 400:
             perturb = normalize_array(best_a + rng.normal(0, 0.004/np.sqrt(n), n))
             a = perturb
             c = convolve_fft(a)
             no = 0
       it +=1
       if it % 200 ==0:
          print(f"  pair it={it} best={best_score:.8f}")
    return best_a, float(best_score)

```python
"""
Hybrid search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2.

The method uses:
  * several analytic/warm-start initializations,
  * a projected gradient / active-set optimization on the simplex,
  * occasional softmax-smoothed gradients to escape flat regions,
  * local pair-coordinate polishing that exactly tracks convolution
    updates and only accepts improvements.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def normalize_array(a):
    """Clip to non-negative, replace non-finite values, normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    np.clip(a, 0.0, None, out=a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def project_simplex(v):
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x) = 1}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(np.where(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[k] - 1.0) / (k + 1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)


def convolve_fft(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a, computed by FFT. Returns length 2n-1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if need <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = 1 << max(0, (need - 1).bit_length())
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)[:need]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def score_array(a):
    """Internal score from normalized array and FFT convolution."""
    a = normalize_array(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    c = convolve_fft(a)
    sc = float(2.0 * n * c.max()) if n > 0 else float("inf")
    return sc, c


def active_gradient(a, idx):
    """
    Gradient of the average of selected convolution coefficients.

    For c_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i},
    d c_k / d a_i = 2 a_{k-i} when valid.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.asarray(idx, dtype=np.int64)
    n = int(a.size)
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    m = int(idx.size)
    if m == 0:
        return g
    for kk in idx:
        k = int(kk)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n, k + 1)
        if lo < hi:
            r = np.arange(lo, hi, dtype=np.int64)
            g[lo:hi] += 2.0 * a[k - r]
    return g / float(m)


def fft_gradient(a, w, N=None):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k c_k.

    If s_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}, then d/d a_i = 2 s_i.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([2.0 * a[0] * w[0]], dtype=np.float64)

    need = n + w.size - 1
    if N is None or int(N) < need:
        N = 1 << max(0, (need - 1).bit_length())

    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(A * W, N)

    # s_i = corr[2n-2-i]
    s = corr[2 * n - 2 : n - 2 : -1].copy()
    g = 2.0 * s
    g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
    return g


def softmax_weights(c, temp):
    """Stable softmax over convolution coefficients."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        if w.size:
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    z = np.exp((c - m) / float(temp))
    s = float(z.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    return z / s


def compute_gradient_top(a, c, top_k, N):
    """Average gradient over the top_k largest convolution coefficients."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = int(c.size)
    top_k = int(max(1, min(top_k, L)))
    if top_k >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:].astype(np.int64)

    if top_k <= 80:
        return active_gradient(a, idx)

    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / float(top_k)
    return fft_gradient(a, w, N)


def gradient_refine(a0, deadline, rng,
                    top_start=16, top_max=4096,
                    soft_fraction=0.35,
                    soft_start=0.04, soft_min=0.001):
    """
    Projected gradient refinement on the simplex.

    For a controlled early part of the run, uses a softmax-smoothed gradient.
    Later uses averages over the active (largest) convolution coefficients.
    Momentum is used conservatively and restarted on stalls.
    """
    a = normalize_array(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return a, float(2.0 * n * a[0] * a[0]) if n == 1 else float("inf")

    c = convolve_fft(a)
    cur_score = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    prev = a.copy()
    beta = 0.0
    accepts_in_row = 0

    top_k = int(top_start)
    scale = 0.015
    no_best = 0
    it = 0

    # Enough FFT size for gradient correlation a * w[::-1].
    N = 1 << max(0, (3 * n - 3).bit_length())

    start = time.monotonic()
    remaining = max(0.0, float(deadline) - start)
    soft_dead = start + remaining * float(soft_fraction)
    soft_temp = float(soft_start)

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        # Optional heavy-ball point.
        if beta > 0.0:
            y = a + beta * (a - prev)
            y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
            y = project_simplex(y)
            c_y = convolve_fft(y)
            score_y = float(2.0 * n * c_y.max())
            if not np.isfinite(score_y) or score_y > cur_score + 1e-8:
                y = a
                c_y = c
                score_y = cur_score
        else:
            y = a
            c_y = c

        # Gradient.
        if time.monotonic() < soft_dead:
            temp = max(float(soft_min), soft_temp * max(1e-15, float(c_y.max())))
            w = softmax_weights(c_y, temp)
            g = fft_gradient(y, w, N)
        else:
            g = compute_gradient_top(y, c_y, top_k, N)

        gmax = float(np.max(g)) if g.size else 0.0
        if (not np.isfinite(gmax)) or gmax < 1e-15:
            beta = 0.0
            no_best += 1
            if no_best > 800:
                a = normalize_array(best + rng.normal(0.0, 0.005 / np.sqrt(n), n))
                c = convolve_fft(a)
                cur_score = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
                prev = a.copy()
                no_best = 0
            continue

        step = scale / (gmax + 1e-12)
        accepted = False

        # Line search on the exact score.
        for _ in range(4):
            cand = project_simplex(y - step * g)
            c_cand = convolve_fft(cand)
            sc = float(2.0 * n * c_cand.max())
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                prev = a.copy()
                a = cand
                c = c_cand
                cur_score = sc
                accepted = True
                if sc < best_score:
                    best = a.copy()
                    best_score = sc
                    no_best = 0
                else:
                    no_best += 1
                break
            step *= 0.5

        if accepted:
            accepts_in_row += 1
            if accepts_in_row >= 3:
                beta = min(0.5, beta + 0.02)
            scale = min(0.25, scale * 1.05)
        else:
            accepts_in_row = 0
            beta = max(0.0, beta * 0.5)
            scale = max(1e-5, scale * 0.6)
            no_best += 1

        it += 1

        if time.monotonic() < soft_dead:
            if it % 500 == 0:
                soft_temp = max(float(soft_min), soft_temp * 0.7)

        if it % 200 == 0:
            if no_best > 100:
                top_k = min(int(top_max), top_k * 2)
                scale = max(scale, 0.02)
                beta = 0.0
                prev = a.copy()
            if no_best > 2000:
                a = normalize_array(best + rng.normal(0.0, 0.006 / np.sqrt(n), n))
                c = convolve_fft(a)
                cur_score = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
                prev = a.copy()
                beta = 0.0
                no_best = 500
            if it % 2000 == 0:
                print(f"  grad it={it} cur={cur_score:.8f} best={best_score:.8f} "
                      f"top={top_k} scale={scale:.5f} beta={beta:.3f}")

    if cur_score < best_score:
        best = a.copy()
        best_score = cur_score
    return best, float(best_score)


def pair_polish(a0, deadline, rng, topK=12, max_pairs=16):
    """
    Local coordinate polishing.

    For a high convolution coefficient k = i + j, try transferring mass
    between a[i] and a[j].  The full affected convolution coefficients are
    updated incrementally; accepted moves are verified by a full FFT.
    """
    a = normalize_array(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    c = convolve_fft(a)
    best_score = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_c = c.copy()

    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    frac_vals = (0.15, 0.35, 0.60, 0.90, 1.00)

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        L = int(c.size)
        if L == 0 or n < 2:
            break

        topK_use = int(max(1, min(topK, L)))
        top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -topK_use)[-topK_use:]

        pairs = []
        seen = set()

        for kk in top_idx:
            k = int(kk)
            lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
            hi = min(n, k + 1)
            if hi - lo < 2:
                continue

            ii = np.arange(lo, hi, dtype=np.int64)
            jj = k - ii
            valid = (ii >= 0) & (ii < n) & (jj >= 0) & (jj < n) & (ii != jj)
            if not np.any(valid):
                continue
            ii = ii[valid]
            jj = jj[valid]
            if ii.size == 0:
                continue

            prod = a[ii] * a[jj]
            m = min(4, int(ii.size))
            if m > 0:
                loc = np.argpartition(prod, -m)[-m:]
                samples = ii[loc].copy()
            else:
                samples = np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)

            if ii.size > 0:
                ridx = rng.integers(0, ii.size, size=2)
                samples = np.concatenate([samples, ii[ridx]])

            for val in samples:
                i = int(val)
                j = int(k - i)
                if i == j or j < 0 or j >= n:
                    continue
                key = (min(i, j), max(i, j))
                if key in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(key)
                pairs.append((i, j))
                if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
                    break
            if len(pairs) >= max_pairs:
                break

        # Fallback random pairs.
        if len(pairs) < max_pairs:
            for _ in range(max_pairs - len(pairs)):
                i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                if i == j:
                    continue
                key = (min(i, j), max(i, j))
                if key in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(key)
                pairs.append((i, j))

        if not pairs:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 100:
                a = normalize_array(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, 0.004 / np.sqrt(n), n))
                c = convolve_fft(a)
                no_improve = 0
            it += 1
            continue

        improved = False

        for i, j in pairs[:max_pairs]:
            x = float(a[i])
            y = float(a[j])
            if x <= 0.0 and y <= 0.0:
                continue

            diff = x - y
            maxamt = min(x, y)
            base = abs(diff)
            if base < 1e-12:
                base = 0.5 * maxamt
            if base <= 0.0:
                continue

            best_delta = None
            best_sc = None
            best_c_new = None

            for frac in frac_vals:
                for sgn in (1.0, -1.0):
                    delta = sgn * base * float(frac)
                    if delta > x:
                        delta = 0.999 * x
                    if delta < -y:
                        delta = -0.999 * y
                    if abs(delta) < 1e-14:
                        continue

                    newc = c.copy()

                    # P_new = P + delta x^i - delta x^j
                    # delta(P^2) = 2 delta (x^i - x^j) P + delta^2 (x^i - x^j)^2
                    newc[i:i + n] += 2.0 * delta * a
                    newc[j:j + n] -= 2.0 * delta * a
                    newc[2 * i] += delta * delta
                    if 2 * j < newc.size:
                        newc[2 * j] += delta * delta
                    newc[i + j] -= 2.0 * delta * delta

                    sc = float(2.0 * n * newc.max())
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                        if best_sc is None or sc < best_sc:
                            best_sc = sc
                            best_delta = delta
                            best_c_new = newc

            if best_delta is not None:
                a[i] += best_delta
                a[j] -= best_delta
                np.maximum(a, 0.0, out=a)

                # Verify the move with a full convolution.
                c_re = convolve_fft(a)
                sc_re = float(2.0 * n * c_re.max())
                if np.isfinite(sc_re) and sc_re < best_score - 1e-13:
                    best_score = sc_re
                    best_a = a.copy()
                    best_c = c_re.copy()
                    c = best_c
                    improved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    # Revert the unverified move.
                    a[i] -= best_delta
                    a[j] += best_delta
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 400:
                a = normalize_array(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, 0.004 / np.sqrt(n), n))
                c = convolve_fft(a)
                no_improve = 0

        it += 1
        if it % 300 == 0:
            print(f"  pair it={it} best={best_score:.8f}")

    return best_a, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Time-bounded search for a non-negative sequence.
    """
    total_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 90.0)
    final_deadline = total_start + budget
    rng = np.random.default_rng(20240406)

    n = 4806

    candidates = []

    # Warm start from previously found construction, if available.
    arr = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        arr = None

    if arr is not None and arr.size > 0:
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
        if arr.size != n:
            old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size)
            new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
            arr = np.interp(new_x, old_x, arr)
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if s > 1e-12:
            candidates.append(arr / s)

    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)

    # One-sided power laws.
    for p in (0.40, 0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60):
        a = idx ** (-p)
        a = a / a.sum()
        candidates.append(a)

    # Regularized endpoint singularities.
    for beta in (0.0, 1.0, 5.0, 20.0):
        a = 1.0 / np.sqrt(idx + beta)
        a = a / a.sum()
        candidates.append(a)

    # Exponential ramp candidates.
    for lam in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        a = np.exp(-lam * np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))
        a = a / a.sum()
        candidates.append(a)

    # Evaluate candidates.
    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    initial_best = None

    for c in candidates:
        a = normalize_array(c)
        sc, _ = score_array(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = a.copy()
            initial_best = a.copy()

    if best is None:
        best = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_score = float(2.0)
        initial_best = best.copy()

    print(f"  initial best score = {best_score:.8f}")
    if best_score < 1.5027:
        print("  initial candidate already below target; continuing to improve.")

    # First gradient phase from a slightly perturbed copy to break symmetry.
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = max(0.0, final_deadline - now)
    if rem > 240.0:
        d1 = 0.45 * rem
        work = normalize_array(best + rng.normal(0.0, 0.015 / np.sqrt(n), n))
        a1, s1 = gradient_refine(
            work,
            now + d1,
            rng,
            top_start=16,
            top_max=4096,
            soft_fraction=0.40,
            soft_start=0.04,
            soft_min=0.001
        )
        if np.isfinite(s1) and s1 < best_score:
            best = a1.copy()
            best_score = float(s1)
        print(f"  after gradient phase 1: {best_score:.8f}")

    # Pair-coordinate polish.
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = max(0.0, final_deadline - now)
    if rem > 160.0:
        d2 = 0.35 * rem
        a2, s2 = pair_polish(best, now + d2, rng, topK=12, max_pairs=16)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
            best = a2.copy()
            best_score = float(s2)
        print(f"  after pair polish: {best_score:.8f}")

    # Final gradient polish.
    now = time.monotonic()
    rem = max(0.0, final_deadline - now)
    if rem > 90.0:
        work = normalize_array(best + rng.normal(0.0, 0.004 / np.sqrt(n), n))
        a3, s3 = gradient_refine(
            work,
            final_deadline,
            rng,
            top_start=64,
            top_max=4096,
            soft_fraction=0.0,
            soft_start=0.01,
            soft_min=0.001
        )
        if np.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_score:
            best = a3.copy()
            best_score = float(s3)
        print(f"  after gradient phase 2: {best_score:.8f}")

    # Final exact evaluation and safe fallback.
    final_candidates = []
    if best is not None:
        final_candidates.append(normalize_array(best))
    if initial_best is not None:
        final_candidates.append(normalize_array(initial_best))
    final_candidates.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))

    best_exact_list = None
    best_exact_score = float("inf")

    for cand in final_candidates:
        if cand is None:
            continue
        lst = [float(x) for x in cand]
        try:
            sc = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = sc
            best_exact_list = lst

    if best_exact_list is None:
        best_exact_list = [float(x) for x in np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)]

    print(f"  final verified score = {best_exact_score:.8f}, length = {len(best_exact_list)}")
    return best_exact_list
```
